<a id="pattern-27"></a>
### 27. Relief can come from time, play, reframing, nursing, a different caregiver or treatment as well as formal training.

**Belongs in:** §§2, 4.2–4.5, 5.5, 9.1, 16.5 and 17.7. **Relation:** Confirms contrary evidence and extends its detail.

**Current conclusion, exact excerpt:** “Time, not method.”

**What should change / remain:** Do not generalize that phrase either: this share includes time, a playful no-cry hand-eating game, a gaming metaphor, less prescribed sleep, spontaneous independent onset, caregiver changes, medication, ongoing nursing and successful CIO. Preserve early versus later outcomes, including a success thread’s later recollection of 39 and 25 minutes rather than an always-under-15-minutes account.

**Full evidence at this location: 29 quotes.**

<a id="sbu01-0094"></a>
**[sbu01-0094]** — partner; regression_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0094 -->
> Whatever happened that night before we are convinced triggered that roll (even if scientifically this is inaccurate, we are using this as part of our coping system). As gamers, my wife and I started joking that morning, "He must has been on a boss level last night, because he spent some serious XP points on that roll-over skill this morning!"
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0095"></a>
**[sbu01-0095]** — partner; regression_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0095 -->
> It may seem silly to other parents, especially non-gamers, but this little shift in mentality has made a significantly positive impact on our mental health. We are finding a way to encourage him while also having fun together. We are acknowledging his new developments come with struggles, and that new skills are earned, not simply granted.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0130"></a>
**[sbu01-0130]** — parent_unspecified; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0130 -->
> I have actually started letting him nap outside recently and he seems to nap better than he does inside lately. We go for a walk in his pram and inevitably he'll drift off and then I park him up in the garden and he'll grab a good little snooze.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0199"></a>
**[sbu01-0199]** — parent_unspecified; looking_back.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0199 -->
> He is now a wonderful toddler full of life and happiness. I’d say he’s very strong willed and not the easiest, but I’m happy with where we are at.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0241"></a>
**[sbu01-0241]** — parent_unspecified; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0241 -->
> Wakes up for the day between 6-7 am & goes to bed between 7:30-8:15 pm. Sleeps through the night 5/7 days of the week.. typically has one false start & then will sleep 8-10 hours straight.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0277"></a>
**[sbu01-0277]** — new_mother; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0277 -->
> He sleeps through the night though, we never formally sleep trained though I planned to (difficult birth, prolonged NICU stay, and now hip dysplasia has pushed back plans).
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0348"></a>
**[sbu01-0348]** — parent_unspecified; regression_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0348 -->
> So I was reading more today and expecting it to go worse…. He babbled to himself for ten minutes, fussed for 5 and cried for 2 and fell asleep.   
> Two. Minutes. Who knows what we are in for the rest of the night/week but I guess I am somewhere between confused and proud.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0046"></a>
**[sbu05-0046]** — experienced_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0046 -->
> My son is a very agreeable, easygoing kid, has a meltdown or screams occasionally but otherwise does his own thing, is very sweet to me, naps great, etc.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0114"></a>
**[sbu05-0114]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0114 -->
> I was doing Ferber for awhile with 6mo to transition from cosleeping, and I was like “yeahh it’s too bad he cries for 10m but then he does sleep for like 8 hours so I guess that’s that,” and you know, Ferber suggests you say goodnight and leave 
> 
> But then turns out all I needed to do, with my particular baby, was do drowsy-but-awake but let him touch my face and pull my hair, and then get him laughing by pretending to eat his hands. No crying at all. Wow.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0115"></a>
**[sbu05-0115]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0115 -->
> Lord knows nothing ever worked before, not binkies, swaddles, preheated bassinet, not the crib, not the Snoo, not the bath and stories before bed, nothing, so this is quite nice. I thought some tears were inevitable. Nope. I just have to pretend to eat his hands like a monster.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0137"></a>
**[sbu05-0137]** — new_mother; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0137 -->
> My baby is now 3 months and she sleeps 1am-7am, and then 8am to 11am. I have adjusted to being a mom, and I have found that I don’t become as frustrated or hard on myself. She is smiling and cooing at me. It brings me so much joy.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0252"></a>
**[sbu05-0252]** — parent_unspecified; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0252 -->
> i just make sure she eats all day every 3 hours and never sleeps more than 2 hours! that helped and she started sleeping through the night around 11 weeks
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0256"></a>
**[sbu05-0256]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0256 -->
> The only difference between this attempt and the last? I didn’t roll her back off her belly. I was terrified even though she can roll both ways and has great head control. Makes sense because my husband is a belly sleeper too. I’ll post an update after tonight and see if last night was a fluke or if belly sleeping is going to be our savior. I’m still putting her down on her back of course, but she immediately rolls to her belly.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0297"></a>
**[sbu05-0297]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0297 -->
> Other than those 2 sleep training attempts we have exclusively coslept and she is still being breastfed before bed. We finally got the courage to try it again after some rough nights cosleeping with frequent wakes.   
> I tried cio for the first time last night and she cried for 1hr 10mins. After laying her in the crib I did not re enter the room. She had one wake at 12:30, cried for 10 mins, and then stayed asleep until I went to get her at 8am!  
> Tonight we put baby in crib and she laid down and went to sleep with 0 tears… I am in shock!!
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0310"></a>
**[sbu05-0310]** — parent_unspecified; baby_9_12_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0310 -->
> Just saw this now!! Yes! Night 2 was worse for us!!! Or maybe it was 3? I think it was 15 mins, 15 mins, then one night was 39 mins, last night was 25 mins
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0330"></a>
**[sbu05-0330]** — parent_unspecified; baby_9_12_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0330 -->
> My daughter is a week away from turning 1 and she is suddenly not wanting to be rocked for bedtime at first. up until yesterday we have rocked her pretty much every single time she’s gone to sleep, but yesterday she was fighting us so i just decided to place her in her crib to see what happened, and she fell asleep on her own. she cried for about a minute or two but then just laid down and rolled around for a while until she dozed off. tonight she did the same thing, but she had a bit more trouble and cried out for me to come rock her after about 30 minutes of her being in the crib on her own self soothing.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0354"></a>
**[sbu05-0354]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0354 -->
> Well suddenly she's like I don't like it anymore and she has self weaned herself. Which is fine and easy, but now sleep has been absolutely terrible until I discovered that by putting a small blanket with her to hug and wrap around herself, she goes to sleep within 10 mins. She slept through the night last night.  I'm in shock, has anyone else's little one comforts been a blanket, and they slept through with one? I'm a bit scared as she's never used a blanket. I've always used sleep sacks.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0019"></a>
**[sbu10-0019]** — new_mother; unknown.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0019 -->
> after hiring help, I am a better mom for my baby. after waking my husband at night just a few times for diaper changes hes stopped asking how is it so hard and why I'm so tired all the time.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0036"></a>
**[sbu10-0036]** — parent_unspecified; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0036 -->
> But the only thing that stops her from sobbing is distracting her, usually by age appropriate “roughhousing”. Spinning her, cradle hold rolling, etc. She’ll giggle and it helps her breathe and sometimes drift to sleep.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0060"></a>
**[sbu10-0060]** — new_mother; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0060 -->
> Yesterday he took the paci well and last night slept (we still co sleep) for 6 hours (the first long stretch EVER)
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0084"></a>
**[sbu10-0084]** — parent_unspecified; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0084 -->
> The only one she started tolerating is the swing where dad sometimes leaves hers to fall asleep and then he moves her for a contact nap. She used to hate the swing as well, but now she falls asleep sometimes in it while looking angry (which is a pretty funny face) for 5 min before dozing off​.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0172"></a>
**[sbu10-0172]** — experienced_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0172 -->
> He used to nurse to sleep up until we weaned at 16 months and then he just started independently sleeping on his own in his crib by himself.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0207"></a>
**[sbu10-0207]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0207 -->
> He is a treat at bedtime, we feed him, brush his teeth, read him a book, then pop him in his crib and he falls asleep by himself within 10 mins minimal crying.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0233"></a>
**[sbu10-0233]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0233 -->
> What we then went to with the help of a sleep consultant was 2hour wake windows (rather than 2.5-2.75) and didn’t cap her naps unless it exceeded 2.5hours. On a few of these days her naps were so long that she only did 2 naps and was awake for 6hours only for the day.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0243"></a>
**[sbu10-0243]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0243 -->
> I wake her up for one night feed and she goes right back down. She essentially sleeps 7:30 - 6 with 3 naps.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0273"></a>
**[sbu10-0273]** — new_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0273 -->
> She needed me to just hold her and audibly breathe calming breaths - that was how she fell asleep after our normal routine failed. And I’m totally okay with it.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0277"></a>
**[sbu10-0277]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0277 -->
> This is what we did: a couple of months before nightweaning, I gave some water in his cup at nightwakes, then BF. So he got used that. Nightwean plan was for dad to go in and give water, say goodnight, leave his room...and see what happens
> 
>   
> Anyway...here's how it went:
> 
> Night 1: woke up, drank water, cried/whined 25m
> 
> Night 2: woke up, drank water, cried 2m
> 
> Night 3: STTN
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0279"></a>
**[sbu10-0279]** — parent_unspecified; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0279 -->
> Yesterday I extended second nap to an hour by cosleeping.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu10-0291"></a>
**[sbu10-0291]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0291 -->
> Last month or so, he started crying and making himself vomit, so we stopped letting him cry for 10m to avoid that, and it's really improved/hasn't happened since then. My partner has also been doing bed time and handling some night wakings to reduce separation anxiety and excessive nursing.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="pattern-28"></a>
### 28. Sleep and care capacity can shape an ongoing pregnancy decision, not just plans for future children.

**Belongs in:** §9.1; add a family-planning and reproductive-decisions subsection. **Relation:** Extends.

**Current conclusion, exact excerpt:** “Plans for more children:”

**What should change / remain:** Keep hypothetical avoidance of another child separate from an existing pregnancy and disagreement about continuing it. Pair the current contact-nap/bedsharing workload with the parent’s stated doubt about capacity, without guessing the eventual decision. Include pregnancy discomfort and fears about returning to work as distinct contexts.

**Full evidence at this location: 24 quotes.**

<a id="sbu01-0062"></a>
**[sbu01-0062]** — experienced_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0062 -->
> (pregnancy and severe post partum depression too).
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0102"></a>
**[sbu01-0102]** — parent_unspecified; looking_back.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0102 -->
> My first baby had a pretty rough start with colic, reflux, and eventually we found out he had a milk protein allergy. Those first few months were honestly some of the hardest of my life. There was a lot of crying, very little sleep, constant worrying, and feeling like I was always trying to figure out what was wrong.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0226"></a>
**[sbu01-0226]** — partner; unknown.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0226 -->
> * **Sleep deprivation:** I have always prioritized getting at least 8 hours of sleep a night. If I do not get that, I tend to feel groggy and easily irritated throughout the day. I am really worried about how I will handle the sleepless nights and constant routine disruptions ahead.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0234"></a>
**[sbu01-0234]** — experienced_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0234 -->
> I have been a stay at home mom since my second daughter was born, and she is going to be two in August. I spent a lot of time today sobbing but not for the potential loss of this pregnancy, but I’m realizing more and more for the loss I have of myself. This weekend was really hard. I’ve been kind of running on little sleep because my second daughter has been having horrible overnights so I’m not sure if that’s the reason I feel like this and the hormones from a potential pregnancy or potential chemical pregnancy.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0237"></a>
**[sbu01-0237]** — expecting_mother; toddler_24_36_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0237 -->
> I’m 7.5 weeks pregnant, nauseous and exhausted as hell. I’ve been SAHM since my 2.5 year old was born. I’m getting burnt out and kind of want to go back to work but I know I don’t want to work when the new baby arrives.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0238"></a>
**[sbu01-0238]** — expecting_mother; toddler_24_36_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0238 -->
> This year has been hard. We moved to a new city away from friends and family, I had a miscarriage in the summer. I’ve lost friends. I’m getting depressed but I’m not on my meds because I’m pregnant. Am I being dramatic or is this just really fucking hard?
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0297"></a>
**[sbu01-0297]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0297 -->
> we are late to the game but finally started sleep training our daughter who just turned 17 months old yesterday (6/30) due to the fact that i'm pregnant and due soon with baby#2. she was doing better than we expected with the ferber method for the last 2 weeks until last night and tonight where she screams and refuses to sleep. her schedule hasn't changed, she wakes around 8-9, naps anywhere between 130-2 until 4-430 then goes down for bed around 830-9. any advice on how to get her to go back to sleeping on her own is appreciated. not sure if she's hitting the 18m regression this early or not. thank you :)
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0325"></a>
**[sbu01-0325]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0325 -->
> I feel so crappy letting him cry if he’s in pain but I am so anxious about co-sleeping with him and we have a baby due in just 3 months.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0392"></a>
**[sbu01-0392]** — parent_unspecified; unknown.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0392 -->

<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0067"></a>
**[sbu05-0067]** — expecting_mother; baby_9_12_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0067 -->

Her solid intake isn’t good, we’re talking just licks of food and her milk is like 600ish in the day, hence when she wakes in the night I can really resettle her without a bottle. What do you do with a toddler?? 😆
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0100"></a>
**[sbu05-0100]** — new_mother; toddler_24_36_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0100 -->

> 
> &#x200B;
> 
> That's not the issue. The issue is that even when he's helping it is not enough. 
> 
> &#x200B;
> 
> He thinks if you know she magically starts sleeping and becoming easier that I will change my mind. I just don't see that happening.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0134"></a>
**[sbu05-0134]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0134 -->
> Seriously thank the stars this kid sleeps like a champ otherwise I can guarantee we would not be having anymore kids. His naps and night sleep are consistently good outside of teething.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0190"></a>
**[sbu05-0190]** — experienced_mother; newborn_0_8_weeks.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0190 -->
> She’s mostly fussy when she’s in the stroller and in the evenings. My husband takes her in the evenings. I just hate that I can’t go out because of how much she hates the stroller and honestly I’m getting enough sleep. I’m more relaxed since this is my second, we cosleep and she’s not the worst sleeper. I have everything yet I’m still feeling like this
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0226"></a>
**[sbu05-0226]** — new_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0226 -->
> The last 15 months of my son’s life have been some of the best, but also some of the most miserable months of my life. I love staying home with him, I love seeing him grow and develop every day and being here for him however I’m also miserable. Every day is the same, I can’t get anything done without him screaming or crying for me, he doesn’t sleep, and I feel like I’m just constantly functioning at 0%. After waiting so long to have children, experiencing multiple miscarriages and years of infertility I thought I would enjoy being a stay at home mom a lot more, but I hate to admit that I don’t enjoy it as much as I wanted to.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0306"></a>
**[sbu05-0306]** — expecting_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0306 -->
> A couple of weeks ago, she learned how to escape her port-a-crib (we've been living with my mom since moving back to my home state in Feb, and have been looking for a house, and we just found one, but won't be moving in until 9/1), and since then, she just wakes whenever she wants, gets out of bed, and won't go back to sleep despite how hard we try. The last two weeks, its been an avg of 5am. Today, 4:20am. I'm 26 weeks pregnant with my 2nd child, a working mom, and losing my mind. My husband helps as much as he can, but she screams bloody murder until I'm up with her and giving her breakfast.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0344 -->

<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu05-0363"></a>
**[sbu05-0363]** — new_mother; toddler_24_36_months.

<!-- /voice-quote -->

<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="pattern-29"></a>
### 29. Parents describe relentless waking, long settling and contact-only sleep even when a day seems otherwise normal.

**Belongs in:** §§1, 2, 6.2 and 9.1. **Relation:** Confirms.

**Current conclusion, exact excerpt:** “She writes at 3am or 5am and posts a log of the night:”

**What should change / remain:** Keep the concrete night and nap accounts, including babies who are happy by day and those distressed by day too. They document the problem and its variability; they do not identify one cause or establish a prevalence rate.

**Full evidence at this location: 30 quotes.**

<a id="sbu01-0026"></a>
**[sbu01-0026]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0026 -->
> My baby is just over 6 months and has always been a light/restless sleeper. Her biggest issue is frequent night waking — often every 1–2 hours, and recently she’s been waking around 3 AM, 4 AM, 5 AM and sometimes staying awake after that.  
> She usually needs to nurse to fall asleep and also to resettle after waking. Rocking/holding often doesn’t work, and she actively searches for the breast. Her naps are also inconsistent, often around 30–40 minutes.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<a id="sbu01-0032"></a>
**[sbu01-0032]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- /voice-quote -->

There is no relief or help. I just feel insane. I just needed to vent because I know my partner and friends are sick of me at this point.

## Outliers and counter-examples

These accounts qualify the main patterns; none is discarded for being inconvenient. The words below are repeated from the pattern evidence for direct comparison.

### Good baby sleep and ample help do not establish psychological recovery.

The ledger identifies the first extract as part of a severe-depression account despite an easy baby and a large support network. Another mother explicitly reports enough sleep, then later improvement attributed to medication; chronic illness can require more sleep than an uninterrupted toddler night allows. This extends §9.1 beyond insomnia after waking improves.

**[sbu10-0111]** — new_mother; looking_back.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0111 -->
> My daughter was (and still is) a good, easy baby. My husband was supportive. I was able to sleep as much as I want. I had a massive village of people willing to take my kiddo whenever I needed it.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0190]** — experienced_mother; newborn_0_8_weeks.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0190 -->
> She’s mostly fussy when she’s in the stroller and in the evenings. My husband takes her in the evenings. I just hate that I can’t go out because of how much she hates the stroller and honestly I’m getting enough sleep. I’m more relaxed since this is my second, we cosleep and she’s not the worst sleeper. I have everything yet I’m still feeling like this
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0191]** — experienced_mother; looking_back.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0191 -->
> I’m feeling sooo much better! My meds took their time to work but then one day something just clicked and I started feeling 1000 times better.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0147]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0147 -->
> My daughter is 15m old and sleeps from 8:30 to 7am everyday. She doesn't wake up during the night. 
> 
> I need a lot of sleep, like at least 10 hours to not feel extremely chronic illness hungover. I do best on 12-14 hours. Because I at least need 10, I go to bed right after she's been put to sleep.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0340]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0340 -->
> But im like fatigued constantly(and I sleep 8hrs most nights??) and just can't wrap my brain around another thing in the routine when its already wake up, breakfast, school drop off, work work work be the boss, make dinner, clean the house, put the baby to bed, go to sleep and wake up and do it again and also never miss a beat while doing all of it 😅
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Independent sleep onset does not ensure a night without repeated waking.

The parent’s objection defeats an interpretation that falling asleep independently is sufficient. Keep the night wakes and the TCB price deterrent together in §§4, 6.2 and 10.

**[sbu01-0288]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0288 -->
> My struggle is this: all the research says your baby will never sleep through the night unless they know how to put themselves to sleep. Unless the way they fell asleep is the same way they wake up. Great. My baby DOES. They put themselves to sleep. We don’t rock all the way to sleep. We don’t nurse to sleep. We nurse. Rock for 5-10 minutes chatting and then into the crib they go wide awake. They can put themselves down. Until it’s 1am. 3am. 5am. Then they’re up screaming until I go in. I’ve resorted to rocking back to sleep the last few months and leaving once asleep but sometimes this can take literally 1-2 hours. The last few nights we’ve tried going in and giving a hug and laying them down and sitting next to the crib. When they cry, reminding them they’re safe and it’s time for bed. We’ve slept on their floor. Nothing works. They wake up screaming and cannot get themselves back down.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu01-0289]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0289 -->
> I almost bought TCB till I saw the price. And read through PLS website but again their foundation is baby will sleep once they put themselves down. My baby does. But still doesn’t sleep. Help lol.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Bedsharing can fail altogether or work better than every attempted alternative.

Retain both directions in §§5.5 and 9.5. One parent says it did not reduce wakes; another child treats the shared bed as play. A different parent reports sleeping through after adopting it because of a dangerous commute. No common causal effect is established by these accounts.

**[sbu01-0137]** — new_mother; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0137 -->
> We’ve actually tried co sleeping and it didn’t help with reducing wake ups. We are so stumped as to why he’s waking so often. Even for contact naps he wakes up as well. Thanks for the suggestion though.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu01-0249]** — parent_unspecified; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0249 -->
> We've tried carriers, strollers, carseat, swing, cosleeping, crib, contact, etc. All the same. Yes he even hates cosleeping. I literally side lie nurse him on the bed he sleeps for 10 mins wakes up screaming.  If I try to cuddle him to sleep he fusses and us wide awake.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0151]** — partner; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0151 -->
> He's currently sick, but that's as of like three days ago and he started this 2-3 weeks ago.
> 
> He currently has every tooth he can have by this point. Some molar cusps are still popping through, but he's never really had trouble sleeping through all of those teeth before, and is totally fine during the day.
> 
> That's the other thing - he *cannot* co-sleep with us. He hates covers, doesn't like being in there with us, and if he does settle he just tries to babble and play with us. It's never worked his whole life lol
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0343]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0343 -->
> This is kinda where I’m at. I work full time and co-sleeping hasn’t worked for us (believe me, I’ve tried). If I put him in our bed he thinks it’s playtime and doesn’t understand he’s supposed to wind down. He just gets overtired and impossible to soothe eventually.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0276]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0276 -->
> Out of desperation,  I was getting so drowsy on my commute to work, I had started to bedshare and she sleeps through the night.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Some goals preserve shared sleep rather than remove it.

Content cannot imply no request: these parents value the arrangement but ask for freedom to leave the bed, better early-morning sleep, or a short first crib stretch.

**[sbu01-0016]** — new_mother; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0016 -->
> This last month and a half she won't let me leave fhe bed or she is awake wifhin 5 minutes even when she basically falls asleep on her own (unlatches, turns away and falls asleep). I have no time for myself or to do chores or to spend with my husband, and I am getting desperate. I don't know what to do, especially since she could do it before. We don't have a crib for her and I don't want to stop bedsharing but I am ready to pull my hair out. Please help - did it pass or do I have to do something for it to pass?
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0017]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0017 -->
> We love bedsharing except for this early morning disruption every single day (happens on a consistent basis) and I guess I am just wondering if others have experienced this or have any advice on how we can get him to sleep more soundly during that part of the early morning.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0174]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0174 -->
> There are positives of cosleeping, I do love waking up to her in the morning and seeing her cute little smile, I love being able to calm her down immediately. But I also I don't sleep very well and the c-curl is not very comfortable. If I could at least get her to sleep a couple hours in the crib, then cosleep that would be great.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Some children improve without the prescribed training pathway; some formal training also succeeds.

Keep spontaneous post-weaning independent sleep, playful no-cry settling, the shorter-window consultant account, and the rapid CIO success. They prevent both a training-only and an anti-training reading.

**[sbu10-0172]** — experienced_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0172 -->
> He used to nurse to sleep up until we weaned at 16 months and then he just started independently sleeping on his own in his crib by himself.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0114]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0114 -->
> I was doing Ferber for awhile with 6mo to transition from cosleeping, and I was like “yeahh it’s too bad he cries for 10m but then he does sleep for like 8 hours so I guess that’s that,” and you know, Ferber suggests you say goodnight and leave 
> 
> But then turns out all I needed to do, with my particular baby, was do drowsy-but-awake but let him touch my face and pull my hair, and then get him laughing by pretending to eat his hands. No crying at all. Wow.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0115]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0115 -->
> Lord knows nothing ever worked before, not binkies, swaddles, preheated bassinet, not the crib, not the Snoo, not the bath and stories before bed, nothing, so this is quite nice. I thought some tears were inevitable. Nope. I just have to pretend to eat his hands like a monster.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0233]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0233 -->
> What we then went to with the help of a sleep consultant was 2hour wake windows (rather than 2.5-2.75) and didn’t cap her naps unless it exceeded 2.5hours. On a few of these days her naps were so long that she only did 2 naps and was awake for 6hours only for the day.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0297]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0297 -->
> Other than those 2 sleep training attempts we have exclusively coslept and she is still being breastfed before bed. We finally got the courage to try it again after some rough nights cosleeping with frequent wakes.   
> I tried cio for the first time last night and she cried for 1hr 10mins. After laying her in the crib I did not re enter the room. She had one wake at 12:30, cried for 10 mins, and then stayed asleep until I went to get her at 8am!  
> Tonight we put baby in crib and she laid down and went to sleep with 0 tears… I am in shock!!
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### A success can contain later qualifications, and relief need not mean no waking.

The ur05 ledger links the first reply to an earlier enthusiastic training success already cited by findings: the later recollection includes 39 and 25 minutes and uncertainty about night order. Do not repeat only the early under-fifteen-minute characterization. Another parent regards two or three brief wakes as sustainable.

**[sbu05-0310]** — parent_unspecified; baby_9_12_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0310 -->
> Just saw this now!! Yes! Night 2 was worse for us!!! Or maybe it was 3? I think it was 15 mins, 15 mins, then one night was 39 mins, last night was 25 mins
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu01-0303]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0303 -->
> Context: my baby slept terribly from 4m until around 12m (15+ wakes per night, early rising, split nights.) At 12m we did a 2 week sleep training schedule (gradual withdrawal method) and calculated his sleep need. We then had a month or so of much better sleep with 2-3 brief wakes on an average night which felt sustainable to us.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Being able to spend money does not make a workable sleeping arrangement available.

The parent willing to buy almost anything cannot safely repeat low pack-and-play transfers and finds every suggested alternative problematic. This qualifies a price-first interpretation of §§8 and 10.

**[sbu05-0085]** — new_mother; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0085 -->
> Here's my problem. The pack & play is so low to the ground I may as well be putting him on the ground. My back and arms cannot do this safely, 10 times a day/night with a 25+ pound baby. I just cant, I am going to end up dropping him or permanently damaging my back. He wakes up every time I try anyway.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0086]** — new_mother; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0086 -->
> I'm also happy to just buy anything that is suitable and safe for him. At this stage money is not the issue,...it's that everything is "unsafe" and "not recommended" for his age: these are the options that apparently are dangerous to do:
> 
> Can I put a mattress in the pack and play to raise it up slightly? It would be flush with the mesh sides and be a "pack and play mattress" so firm, breathable etc. Maybe 2 of them?
> 
> Or could I get those rails that attach to queen beds that would prevent him from rolling off the bed? 
> 
> Or should I just buy a toddler floor bed, baby proof the whole room, donate the queen bed & all the clothes stored underneath it? Thing is, apparently the toddler beds are climbing hazards and not suitable for babies under 1.
> 
> Mattress on the floor will grow mold and he'll just roll onto the floor
> 
> Please let me know what you have done that worked and was safe? Like safe enough, we can only do so much right?!
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### The caregiver’s interpretation can change without a sleep technique changing.

The gaming reframe acknowledges uncertain science; a father’s childhood-insomnia reflection reframes his son’s cry. These are reported coping shifts, not proof of a child-sleep mechanism.

**[sbu01-0094]** — partner; regression_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0094 -->
> Whatever happened that night before we are convinced triggered that roll (even if scientifically this is inaccurate, we are using this as part of our coping system). As gamers, my wife and I started joking that morning, "He must has been on a boss level last night, because he spent some serious XP points on that roll-over skill this morning!"
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu01-0095]** — partner; regression_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0095 -->
> It may seem silly to other parents, especially non-gamers, but this little shift in mentality has made a significantly positive impact on our mental health. We are finding a way to encourage him while also having fun together. We are acknowledging his new developments come with struggles, and that new skills are earned, not simply granted.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu01-0228]** — partner; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0228 -->
> Man, what you said about generational cycles just hit me like a ton of bricks. Growing up around age 7, I struggled with severe insomnia. My dad couldn't handle it and would spank me just so I'd stop coming into his room and bothering him while he slept. The pain and fear kept me away, and I was left alone in my bed dealing with pure panic until 3 AM.
> Reading your comment made me realize why my son's nighttime meltdowns trigger such a visceral, intense panic/stress response in me. The evening was coded as a time of rejection and distress for me as a kid.
> Reframing his cry as 'I'm uncomfortable and need my dad' instead of feeling triggered changes everything. Thank you so much for shedding light on this—it gives me a clear direction to dig deeper and actually heal this. Truly appreciate it, brother. Love you.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Care and assessment may remain inconclusive, and source statistics may be unsupported.

Normal iron after supplementation, ear tubes and normal specialist tests leave waking unresolved. Separately, the extraordinary mortality wording below is an author’s assertion, as the ur10 ledger states, not a finding about mortality.

**[sbu01-0293]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0293 -->
> I had his iron checked and it was very low so our doctor had us do two months of iron supplements to get it back to normal. He also had chronic recurring ear infections so he’s now had ear tubes in. Everyone said that after tubes their kids slept so much better, and his iron is normal now, but still his sleep is so horrible. We’re cosleeping and he wakes me up 4-8 times a night to help him get back to sleep. He starts in his own crib after screaming to sleep and stays in his crib 2-5 hours before coming to my bed.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu01-0318]** — partner; baby_9_12_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0318 -->
> We have also been pretty thorough medically. We’ve talked to his pediatrician multiple times, seen specialists, had blood work done including iron/ferritin, and so far everything has been normal.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0141]** — advisor; unknown.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0141 -->
> Stop being afraid of 'unpreventable' SIDS, vaccines and formula.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0142]** — advisor; unknown.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0142 -->
> 99% of babies will die bedsharing, with blankets, pillows bumpers, toys, swaddled too long or propped on incline, sleeping in a swing, placed not on their back.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

## What fits nowhere

The current findings have broad adjacent sections, but no dedicated treatment of the following mechanisms or practical tasks. These are proposed additions, not reasons to omit evidence. Full passages are repeated here where the gap is most explicit.

### Caregiver neurodivergence and execution of a method

Add adult sensory overload and routine/executive-function limits alongside the child’s temperament. A quiet, dark, contact-only nap can itself be intolerable to the caregiver.

**[sbu01-0012]** — experienced_mother; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0012 -->
> I’m highly sensory sensitive to touch and sound which as you can imagine is not helped at all in this situation. 🙄😖 Probably plays into some of my frustration.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu01-0196]** — new_mother; baby_2_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0196 -->
> I typically only get him to nap if he lays on my chest with the room dark and only white noise on. Which leads me to be trapped with a baby on my chest for the duration of the nap. It’s not ideal for my mental health. As the title says, I’m AuDHD and have a lot of sensory struggles.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu01-0245]** — parent_unspecified; baby_9_12_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0245 -->
> we cosleep and we don’t have a super solid bedtime routine, we try to dim the lights about an hour before bedtime, we’ll read some books, just trying to wind down and set the mood but i wouldn’t say we’re extremely consistent (sleep deprived ADHD parents that struggle with routine).
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Homelessness, disaster displacement and loss of the other caregiver

§9.6 covers constrained rooms, but a car, an anticipated tent, flood displacement and a partner’s death remove assumptions more fundamental than bedroom size. A return to safe family surroundings after domestic violence is a different kind of sleep improvement from a schedule fix.

**[sbu10-0315]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0315 -->
> I think it will be easier for all of us once we get the tent, especially because we can have a small section for the kids toys, they’ll both have a real mattress again, and my husband and I will be able to get some real sleep again. The kids have been having a good time throughout the day, but at night they start to struggle a lot. Our one year old has been waking up every few hours, and our 4 year old is having a lot of anxiety and keeps asking for her bed. It’s so hard because no matter what we say to reassure her that she’ll have her bed back soon, I can see how worried she still is.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0121]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0121 -->
> My 6mo old was never \~the best\~ sleeper but now we had no choice but to cosleep as we temporarily stayed with family who had no sleep space for baby.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0122]** — parent_unspecified; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0122 -->
> Now with the flood he has been going to sleep at all different times and no really solid consistent routine since I have to be in bed with him for the night due to the cosleeping situation. He ended up awake pretty late some nights bc without blackout curtains it doesnt get dark until almost 9pm by me.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0014]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0014 -->
> My amazing and involved husband passed away very unexpectedly in the spring, leaving me with our two babies (ages 3 and 1).
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0015]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0015 -->
> How do I handle bedtime for both? (Currently co-sleeping with baby, toddler sleeps in own room but needs me to lay with her).
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0171]** — experienced_mother; unknown.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0171 -->
> My kids will never sleep afraid for their lives again. Tonight I think we all sleep better than we have in weeks.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Loss history within a crying limit

Add the surviving-child account and delayed holding after NICU cooling therapy. The stated distinction is supported crying versus being left alone, not simply gentle parenting as a segment.

**[sbu01-0346]** — new_mother; baby_9_12_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0346 -->
> Full cry-it-out feels emotionally really hard for me because we unexpectedly lost our baby girl shortly after birth a little over two years ago. My final memories of her involve hearing her scream as she was taken away from me while I was unable to comfort her. Hearing my son scream can bring back flashbacks. I can handle some supported crying, but leaving him alone to scream feels very challenging.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0026]** — new_mother; baby_5_8_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0026 -->
> My baby had hypoxia at birth and was in the NICU for the first 9 days of her life, under cooling therapy and sedated. I couldn’t hold her until day 5, nursed her on day 6. 
> 
> From this experience I feel guilt that she suffered ANY trauma and honestly hate to see her under any kind of duress whatsoever.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0119]** — new_mother; newborn_0_8_weeks.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0119 -->
> I should also add we had a full term stillbirth one year ago and that adds to my fear and anxiety of doing anything to cause harm.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### A live reproductive decision

The parent’s current sleep/contact workload and her doubt about continuing an unexpected pregnancy belong together. This goes beyond the existing future-children examples; no outcome is inferred.

She still only contact naps, and co sleeps at night. Which means I have practically no time for my self at all.

Add privacy and measurement definitions to §4.3; neither is captured by tracking helping or worsening anxiety.

<!-- /voice-quote -->

<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0325]** — parent_unspecified; regression_4_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0325 -->
> I guess what I still struggle with is if my 4 month old will only nap 20-30mins at a time, but took 10-15mins to fall asleep, then it’s being counted as a 30-45min nap but he’s barely sleeping even that. I feel like then following with the full 2hr wake window is just wrecking him and we’re stuck in an overtired loop
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Changing heat across the night

A static temperature field cannot represent this family’s reported sequence. Keep the unresolved consultation/testing account with the sequence rather than declaring a cause.

**[sbu05-0346]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0346 -->
> We gradually turn on heating 30-60 minutes after she falls asleep, otherwise she gets cold and wakes up crying. You would ask how we know she is cold - she feels cold at back, chest, hands. During the night the temperature has to rise 2-3-4 degrees C and we usually put a light blanket on top of her, otherwise she wakes up all the time. So it is getting warmer where we live now, but up until recently in winter, she would fall asleep at 20-21 degrees C and the temperature would have to get to 23-24 C or she would wake up all the time. We have noticed that 2,5-3 hours after falling asleep she would get cold in like 15 minutes. I check her back -warm - 15 minutes later feels very cold.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0347]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0347 -->
> We took her to several doctors to ask. Our family doctor said that he did not know what to suggest and referred us to others. He did mention that it is normal for body temperature to drop in deep sleep (I read about it too) but not to wake up because of it. Other two doctors were terrible... Suggested we let her just cry... keep the room temperature stable all night(does not work)... ditch the sleeping bag and let her kick her blanket (she would be even COLDER)... because apparently we should not be scared to let her get cold as she would not get sick from it (never have I said getting sick was the problem)... It was very frustrating. We did some lab tests (hormones and ferritin) - 2 out of three doctors said were not concerning, the third didn't want to look... To me(not a doctor) and from what I read online, ferritin could be a little low. Doctors say not anemic and that's it.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

Add the practical 24–36-month tasks: backwards-sack escape, clearing the room, bed-rail gaps, nighttime potty access and what to do when a child leaves bed. Broad cot-climbing references are not yet a treatment of these questions.

### Family structure and divided evenings

Give the two-mother account its own words. Nursing, a wife and shared chores do not fit a father-versus-mother shorthand for every household.

**[sbu10-0341]** — new_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu10-0341 -->
> After dinner, there's a non-negotiable 20 min walk with baby to give my wife some time to just chill on the couch, then she gets the baby ready for bed while I do the dishes. Then I nurse her to sleep and either do more dishes or go to bed.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Contact as compensation, not only a settling strategy

The mother links lost breastfeeding after drinking with an effort to close a bonding gap through constant contact, naps and shared sleep. Preserve the alcohol, guilt and attachment sequence in her words.

**[sbu05-0012]** — new_mother; looking_back.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0012 -->
> I was so depressed and no sleep after my baby born. Also crazy colicky. My milk was coming A LOTTTTT! My freezer was so full like I can't describe my milk was squirting from my breasts. But then... I started to drink alcohol in those times. That was the only moments I felt sane and normal and felt joy. Than I started to drink more and more every day but I stopped giving milk to her right away, I waited around six hours because my milk was contamitated with alcohol. So mostly I was giving bottle instead of breastfeeding.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0013]** — new_mother; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0013 -->
> I don't know is it because of that but I am giving extra extra extra love to my baby nonstop, like trying to close that bonding gap. Always on me, always still contact nap, bad sharing at the night (we're hugging a lottttt like we're sleeping almost as a one body) like my every second is with her.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

### Scope-edge and small contextual fragments

Keep a retrospective six-year-old treatment outcome labelled as retrospective, not evidence about medication for an infant. A brief old-infant-sleep description in an older-child thread, an otherwise nonspecific newborn/toddler routine, and broad developmental comparisons are context rather than independent evidence of a sleep intervention. Do not invent an age, symptom or result to make these fragments fit more tightly.

**[sbu01-0164]** — experienced_mother; looking_back.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu01-0164 -->
> Well, it has been over 6 years, and he sleeps now. Not because of some amazing training technique. Not because we found the perfect schedule and stick to it religiously. Sadly, not even because he just got older and just started sleeping through the night independently (though things did get better than the newborn/ baby phase for sure!!). No, he reliably falls asleep within 10-20 minutes and stays asleep through the night now because he is prescribed long-acting non-stimulant medication for ADHD 😬
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0360]** — parent_unspecified; looking_back.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0360 -->
> Shitty sleeper in that they took forever to sleep through the night as a baby,
<!-- /voice-quote -->

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0365 -->
> Wake up, breakfast, play, lunch, nap, play, outside, dinner, bathtime witching hour, bed, its just so tedious.
<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu05-0370]** — parent_unspecified; toddler_12_24_months.

<!-- voice-quote:sbu05-0370 -->

<!-- /voice-quote -->

**[sbu10-0338]** — experienced_mother; baby_5_8_months.

There are **no stated numeric monetary prices, no refund accounts and no money-back claims in these 1,121 quote fields**. Ages, hours, weights, temperatures, percentages and medicine amounts are not prices. The share contains two records tagged price_signal and thirteen tagged purchase, but this broader inventory catches statements outside those tags too. It supplies no acceptance or rejection of Mamivio’s particular price. Actual care hours, a tool purchase, a contemplated nanny and a digital plan are separate purchases or decisions.

## Requests

These links point to complete source wording above. The first index identifies product, concrete plan/list/protocol and service questions, including modifications of existing plans, intended professional requests and specific desired arrangements. The second retains other questions, appeals, solidarity and desired outcomes so nothing disappears because extraction tags or question marks were inconsistent. Neither inventory is a count of demand for Mamivio or willingness to buy it.

### Other direct appeals, source questions and desired outcomes

This deliberately broad audit index includes rhetorical questions and goals stated without an explicit product/service ask; those are not promoted into purchase intent. A quote with an embedded request tagged pain, fear, belief or failure_story remains visible here or in the concrete indexes above.

### Request limits the rewrite should retain

Parents specify conditions before asking: keep bedsharing; retain daytime contact/nursing while changing nights; avoid leaving the room or prolonged crying; work with a swallowing disorder, bottle refusal, no spare bedroom, no other caregiver, a disabled body or another child. A request can be for safe furniture, an actual care shift, a referral, a timestamped example, privacy, or companionship. An appeal for help is not evidence that one questionnaire document meets that need. All these constraints remain in the linked complete passages.
